Following further legislation shortly afterwards three further provinces were created from three of the original six, leaving the country with nine provinces in 1862, each with a degree of autonomy under an elected [superintendent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintendent_(New_Zealand) "Superintendent (New Zealand)").[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-nine_provinces-188) Because of financial pressures and the desire to consolidate railways, education, land sales, and other policies, government was centralised and the provinces were abolished in 1876.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-189) The provinces are remembered in [regional public holidays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_New_Zealand "Public holidays in New Zealand")[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-190) and sporting rivalries.[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-191)

Since 1876, various councils have administered local areas under legislation determined by the central government.[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-nine_provinces-188)[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-192) In 1989, the government reorganised [local government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_New_Zealand "Local government in New Zealand") into the current two-tier structure of [regional councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_New_Zealand "Regions of New Zealand") and [territorial authorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_authorities "Territorial authorities").[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Sancton2000-193) The [249 municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_former_territorial_authorities_in_New_Zealand "List of former territorial authorities in New Zealand")[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Sancton2000-193) that existed in 1975 have been consolidated into 67 territorial authorities and 11 regional councils.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-194) The regional councils' role is to regulate "the natural environment with particular emphasis on [resource management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991 "Resource Management Act 1991")",[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Sancton2000-193) while territorial authorities are responsible for sewage, water, local roads, building consents, and other local matters.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmeltJui_Lin200933-195)[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-glossary-196) Five of the territorial councils are [unitary authorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_authority "Unitary authority") and also act as regional councils.[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-glossary-196) The territorial authorities consist of 13 city councils, 53 [district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_New_Zealand "Districts of New Zealand") councils, and the [Chatham Islands Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chatham_Islands_Council "Chatham Islands Council"). While officially the Chatham Islands Council is not a unitary authority, it undertakes many functions of a regional council.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-197)

The Realm of New Zealand, one of 15 [Commonwealth realms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_realm "Commonwealth realm"),[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-198) is the entire area over which the king or queen of New Zealand is [sovereign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign "Sovereign") and comprises New Zealand, [Tokelau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokelau "Tokelau"), the [Ross Dependency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ross_Dependency "Ross Dependency"), the [Cook Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_Islands "Cook Islands"), and [Niue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niue "Niue").[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-GG_constitution-156) The Cook Islands and Niue are self-governing states in [free association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associated_state "Associated state") with New Zealand.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-199)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-200) The New Zealand Parliament cannot pass legislation for these countries, but with their consent can act on behalf of them in foreign affairs and defence. Tokelau is classified as a [non-self-governing territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories "United Nations list of non-self-governing territories"), but is administered by a council of three elders (one from each Tokelauan [atoll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atoll "Atoll")).[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-201) The Ross Dependency is New Zealand's [territorial claim in Antarctica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_claims_in_Antarctica "Territorial claims in Antarctica"), where it operates the [Scott Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Base "Scott Base") research facility.[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-202) [New Zealand nationality law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_nationality_law "New Zealand nationality law") treats all parts of the realm equally, so most people born in New Zealand, the Cook Islands, Niue, Tokelau, and the Ross Dependency are New Zealand citizens.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-203)[\[n 8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-205)

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_New_Zealand "Foreign relations of New Zealand")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/P20220531AS-0492-2_%2852245764500%29.jpg/250px-P20220531AS-0492-2_%2852245764500%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:P20220531AS-0492-2_(52245764500).jpg) Former New Zealand prime minister [Jacinda Ardern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacinda_Ardern "Jacinda Ardern") with US president [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") in the [Oval Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Office "Oval Office"), 2022

During the period of the New Zealand colony, Britain was responsible for external trade and foreign relations.[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-206) The 1923 and 1926 [Imperial Conferences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Conference "Imperial Conference") decided that New Zealand should be allowed to negotiate its own political [treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaties "Treaties"), and the first commercial treaty was ratified in 1928 with Japan. On 3 September 1939, New Zealand allied itself with Britain and [declared war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_war "Declaration of war") on Germany with Prime Minister [Michael Joseph Savage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Joseph_Savage "Michael Joseph Savage") proclaiming, "Where she goes, we go; where she stands, we stand".[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-207)

[![A squad of men kneel in the desert sand while performing a war dance](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/E_003261_E_Maoris_in_North_Africa_July_1941.jpg/250px-E_003261_E_Maoris_in_North_Africa_July_1941.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:E_003261_E_Maoris_in_North_Africa_July_1941.jpg) [Māori Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_Battalion "Māori Battalion") [haka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haka "Haka") in Egypt, 1941

In 1951, New Zealand, Australia, and the US signed the ANZUS Treaty.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-208)[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-209) The influence of the United States on New Zealand weakened following protests over the [Vietnam War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_in_the_Vietnam_War "New Zealand in the Vietnam War"),[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-210) the refusal of the United States to admonish France after the [sinking of the _Rainbow Warrior_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinking_of_the_Rainbow_Warrior "Sinking of the Rainbow Warrior"),[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-211) disagreements over environmental and agricultural trade issues, and [New Zealand's nuclear-free policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_nuclear-free_zone "New Zealand nuclear-free zone").[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-212)[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-213) Despite the United States's suspension of ANZUS obligations, the treaty remained in effect between New Zealand and Australia, whose foreign policy has followed a similar historical trend.[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-214) Close political contact is maintained between the two countries, with [free trade agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closer_Economic_Relations "Closer Economic Relations") and [travel arrangements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Tasman_Travel_Arrangement "Trans-Tasman Travel Arrangement") that allow citizens to visit, live and work in both countries without restrictions.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-NZ_in_brief-215)In 2013[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=New_Zealand&action=edit) there were about 650,000 New Zealand citizens living in Australia, which is equivalent to 15% of the population of New Zealand.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-216)

New Zealand has a strong presence among the [Pacific Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Island "Pacific Island") countries, and enjoys strong diplomatic relations with [Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoa "Samoa"), [Fiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiji "Fiji"), and [Tonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonga "Tonga"), and among smaller nations.[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-217) A large proportion of New Zealand's aid goes to these countries, and many Pacific people migrate to New Zealand for employment. The increase of this since the 1960s led to the formation of the [Pasifika New Zealander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasifika_New_Zealanders "Pasifika New Zealanders") pan-ethnic group, the fourth-largest ethnic grouping in the country.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-218)[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-219) Permanent migration is regulated under the 1970 Samoan Quota Scheme and the 2002 Pacific Access Category, which allow up to 1,100 Samoan nationals and up to 750 other Pacific Islanders respectively to become permanent New Zealand residents each year. A seasonal workers scheme for temporary migration was introduced in 2007, and in 2009 about 8,000 Pacific Islanders were employed under it.[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-220) New Zealand is involved in the [Pacific Islands Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum "Pacific Islands Forum"), the [Pacific Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Community "Pacific Community"), [Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation "Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation"), and the [Association of Southeast Asian Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Southeast_Asian_Nations "Association of Southeast Asian Nations") Regional Forum (including the [East Asia Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asia_Summit "East Asia Summit")).[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-NZ_in_brief-215) New Zealand has been described as a [middle power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_power "Middle power") in the Asia-Pacific region,[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-221) and an [emerging power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerging_power "Emerging power").[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-222)[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-223) The country is a member of the [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_and_the_United_Nations "New Zealand and the United Nations"),[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-224) the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations "Commonwealth of Nations")[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-225) and the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development "Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development") (OECD),[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-226) and participates in the [Five Power Defence Arrangements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Power_Defence_Arrangements "Five Power Defence Arrangements").[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-227)

Today, New Zealand enjoys [particularly close relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand%E2%80%93United_States_relations "New Zealand–United States relations") with the United States and is one of its [major non-NATO allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_non-NATO_ally "Major non-NATO ally"),[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-:0-228) as [well as with Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93New_Zealand_relations "Australia–New Zealand relations"), with a " [Trans-Tasman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Tasman "Trans-Tasman")" identity between citizens of the latter being common.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Lynch2009-229) New Zealand is a member of the [Five Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Eyes "Five Eyes") intelligence sharing agreement, known formally as the [UKUSA Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UKUSA_Agreement "UKUSA Agreement"). The five members of this agreement comprise the [core Anglosphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglosphere "Anglosphere"): Australia, Canada, New Zealand, the United Kingdom, and the United States.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-230) Since 2012, New Zealand has had a partnership arrangement with [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty_Organization "North Atlantic Treaty Organization") under the Partnership Interoperability Initiative.[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-231)[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-232)[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-233) According to the 2024 [Global Peace Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Peace_Index "Global Peace Index"), New Zealand is the 4th most peaceful country in the world.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-234)

### Military

Main article: [New Zealand Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Defence_Force "New Zealand Defence Force")

[![A soldier in a green army uniform faces forward](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/ANZAC_Day_service_at_the_National_War_Memorial_-_Flickr_-_NZ_Defence_Force_%2820%29.jpg/250px-ANZAC_Day_service_at_the_National_War_Memorial_-_Flickr_-_NZ_Defence_Force_%2820%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ANZAC_Day_service_at_the_National_War_Memorial_-_Flickr_-_NZ_Defence_Force_(20).jpg) [Anzac Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anzac_Day "Anzac Day") service at the National War Memorial

New Zealand's military services—the New Zealand Defence Force—comprise the [New Zealand Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Army "New Zealand Army"), the [Royal New Zealand Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_New_Zealand_Air_Force "Royal New Zealand Air Force") and the [Royal New Zealand Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_New_Zealand_Navy "Royal New Zealand Navy").[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-235) New Zealand's [national defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_defence "National defence") needs are modest since a direct attack is unlikely.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-236) However, its military has [had a global presence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_New_Zealand "Military history of New Zealand"). The country fought in both world wars, with notable campaigns in [Gallipoli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallipoli_Campaign "Gallipoli Campaign"), [Crete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Crete "Battle of Crete"),[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-237) [El Alamein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Battle_of_El_Alamein "Second Battle of El Alamein"),[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-238) and [Monte Cassino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Monte_Cassino "Battle of Monte Cassino").[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-239) The Gallipoli campaign played an important part in fostering New Zealand's [national identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_identity "National identity")[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-240)[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-241) and strengthened the [ANZAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_and_New_Zealand_Army_Corps "Australian and New Zealand Army Corps") tradition it shares with Australia.[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-242)

In addition the two world wars, New Zealand fought in the [Second Boer War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Boer_War "Second Boer War"),[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-243) the [Korean War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_in_the_Korean_War "New Zealand in the Korean War"),[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-244) the [Malayan Emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayan_Emergency "Malayan Emergency"),[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-245) the [Vietnam War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_War "Vietnam War"), the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War "Gulf War"), and the [Afghanistan War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Afghanistan_(2001%E2%80%93present) "War in Afghanistan (2001–present)"). It has contributed forces to several regional and global peacekeeping missions, such as those in [Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus_dispute "Cyprus dispute"), [Somalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somali_Civil_War "Somali Civil War"), [Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_War "Bosnian War"), the [Sinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suez_Crisis "Suez Crisis"), [Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angolan_Civil_War "Angolan Civil War"), [Cambodia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodian%E2%80%93Vietnamese_War "Cambodian–Vietnamese War"), the [Iran–Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War "Iran–Iraq War") border, [Bougainville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bougainville_campaign "Bougainville campaign"), [East Timor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Astute "Operation Astute"), and the [Solomon Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon_Islands#Civil_war "Solomon Islands").[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-246)

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_New_Zealand "Economy of New Zealand")

See also: [List of companies of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_of_New_Zealand "List of companies of New Zealand")

[![Boats docked in blue-green water. Plate glass skyscrapers rising up in the background.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Auckland_Waterfrt.jpg/250px-Auckland_Waterfrt.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Auckland_Waterfrt.jpg) [Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auckland_waterfront "Auckland waterfront") along [Auckland CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auckland_CBD "Auckland CBD"), a major hub of economic activity

New Zealand has an [advanced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_economy "Advanced economy") [market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_economy "Market economy"),[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-247) ranked 16th in the 2022[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=New_Zealand&action=edit) [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index "Human Development Index"),[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-248) and fourth in the 2022[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=New_Zealand&action=edit) [Index of Economic Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Index_of_Economic_Freedom "Index of Economic Freedom").[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-249) It is a [high-income economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-income_economy "High-income economy") with a [nominal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nominal_value "Nominal value") gross domestic product (GDP) per capita (in United States dollar equivalents) of $48,071 as of 2023.[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-IMFWEO.NZ-15)The currency is the [New Zealand dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_dollar "New Zealand dollar"), informally known as the "Kiwi dollar"; it also circulates in the Cook Islands (see [Cook Islands dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_Islands_dollar "Cook Islands dollar")), Niue, Tokelau, and the [Pitcairn Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitcairn_Islands "Pitcairn Islands").[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-250)

Historically, extractive industries have contributed strongly to New Zealand's economy, focusing at different times on sealing, whaling, [flax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phormium "Phormium"), gold, [kauri gum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kauri_gum "Kauri gum"), and native timber.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-RWT_export_evolution-251) The first shipment of refrigerated meat on the _[Dunedin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunedin_(1874_ship) "Dunedin (1874 ship)")_ in 1882 led to the establishment of meat and dairy exports to Britain, a trade which provided the basis for strong economic growth in New Zealand.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-252) High demand for agricultural products from the United Kingdom and the United States helped New Zealanders achieve higher living standards than both Australia and Western Europe in the 1950s and 1960s.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-253) In 1973, New Zealand's export market was reduced when the United Kingdom joined the [European Economic Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Economic_Community "European Economic Community")[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-254) and other compounding factors, such as the [1973 oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_oil_crisis "1973 oil crisis") and [1979 energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1979_oil_crisis "1979 oil crisis") crises, led to a severe [economic depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Depression_(economics) "Depression (economics)").[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-255) Living standards in New Zealand fell behind those of Australia and Western Europe, and by 1982 New Zealand had the lowest per-capita income of all the developed nations surveyed by [the World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank_Group "World Bank Group").[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-256) In the mid-1980s New Zealand deregulated [its agricultural sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_New_Zealand "Agriculture in New Zealand") by phasing out [subsidies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_subsidy "Agricultural subsidy") over a three-year period.[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-257)[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-258) Since 1984, successive governments engaged in major [macroeconomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macroeconomic "Macroeconomic") restructuring (known first as [Rogernomics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rogernomics "Rogernomics") and then [Ruthanasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruthanasia "Ruthanasia")), rapidly transforming New Zealand from a [protectionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism "Protectionism") and highly regulated economy to a liberalised [free-trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-trade "Free-trade") economy.[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Liberalisation-259)[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-260)

[![Blue water against a backdrop of snow-capped mountains](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/MilfordSound.jpg/250px-MilfordSound.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:MilfordSound.jpg) [Milford Sound / Piopiotahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milford_Sound_/_Piopiotahi "Milford Sound / Piopiotahi") is one of New Zealand's most famous tourist destinations.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-261)

Unemployment peaked just above 10% in 1991 and 1992,[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-unemployment-262) following the [1987 share market crash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Monday_(1987)#New_Zealand "Black Monday (1987)"), but eventually fell to 3.7% in 2007 (ranking third from twenty-seven comparable OECD nations).[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-unemployment-262) However, the [2008 financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_financial_crisis "2008 financial crisis") had a major effect on New Zealand, with the GDP shrinking for five consecutive quarters, the longest recession in over thirty years,[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-263)[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-264) and unemployment rising back to 7% in late 2009.[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-265) The lowest unemployment rate recorded using the current methodology was in December 2021 during the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic"), at 3.2%.[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-266) Unemployment rates for different age groups follow similar trends but are consistently higher among youth. During the September 2025 quarter, the general unemployment rate was around 5.3%, while the unemployment rate for youth aged 15 to 24 was 15.2%.[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-267)[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-unemployment-262)[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-268) New Zealand has experienced a series of " [brain drains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_capital_flight "Human capital flight")" since the 1970s[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-269) that still continue today.[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-270) Nearly one-quarter of highly skilled workers live overseas, mostly in Australia and Britain, which is the largest proportion from any developed nation.[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-271) In recent decades, however, a " [brain gain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_capital_flight "Human capital flight")" has brought in educated professionals from Europe and less developed countries.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-272)[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain200644-273) Today New Zealand's economy benefits from a high level of [innovation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innovation "Innovation").[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-274)

[Poverty in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_New_Zealand "Poverty in New Zealand") is characterised by growing income inequality; wealth in New Zealand [is highly concentrated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_inequality_in_New_Zealand "Economic inequality in New Zealand"),[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-275) with the top 1% of the population owning 16% of the country's wealth, and the richest 5% owning 38%, leaving a stark contrast where half the population, including [state beneficiaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_in_New_Zealand "Welfare in New Zealand") and pensioners, receive less than $24,000.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-276) Moreover, [child poverty in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_poverty_in_New_Zealand "Child poverty in New Zealand") has been identified by the government as a major societal issue;[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Chpov-277)[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-278) the country has 12.0% of children living in low-income households that have less than 50% of the median equivalised disposable household income as of June 2022[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=New_Zealand&action=edit).[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-279) Poverty has a disproportionately high effect in ethnic-minority households, with a quarter (23.3%) of Māori children and almost a third (28.6%) of Pacific Islander children living in poverty as of 2020[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=New_Zealand&action=edit).[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Chpov-277)

### Trade

New Zealand is heavily dependent on international trade,[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-280) particularly in agricultural products.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-281) Exports account for 24% of its output,[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-CIA-102) making New Zealand vulnerable to international commodity prices and global [economic slowdowns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recession "Recession"). Food products made up 55% of the value of all the country's exports in 2014; wood was the second largest earner (7%).[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-282) [New Zealand's main trading partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_largest_trading_partners_of_New_Zealand "List of the largest trading partners of New Zealand"), as at June 2018[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=New_Zealand&action=edit), are China (NZ$27.8b), Australia ($26.2b), the European Union ($22.9b), the United States ($17.6b), and Japan ($8.4b).[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-GSTC_StatNZ-283) On 7 April 2008, New Zealand and China signed the [New Zealand–China Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand%E2%80%93China_Free_Trade_Agreement "New Zealand–China Free Trade Agreement"), the first such agreement China has signed with a developed country.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-284) In July 2023, New Zealand and the European Union entered into the [EU–New Zealand Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand%E2%80%93European_Union_relations#EU-NZ_free_trade_agreement "New Zealand–European Union relations"), which eliminated tariffs on several goods traded between the two regions.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-285) This free trade agreement expanded on the pre-existing free trade agreement[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-286) and saw a reduction in tariffs on meat and dairy[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-287) in response to feedback from the affected industries.[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-288)

The service sector is the largest sector in the economy, followed by manufacturing and construction and then farming and raw material extraction.[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-CIA-102) [Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_New_Zealand "Tourism in New Zealand") plays a significant role in the economy, contributing $12.9 billion (or 5.6%) to New Zealand's total GDP and supporting 7.5% of the total workforce in 2016.[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-TourismStats-289)

[![A Romney ewe with her two lambs](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Fauna_de_Nueva_Zelanda07.JPG/250px-Fauna_de_Nueva_Zelanda07.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Fauna_de_Nueva_Zelanda07.JPG) Wool has historically been one of New Zealand's major exports.

Wool was New Zealand's major agricultural export during the late 19th century.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-RWT_export_evolution-251) Even as late as the 1960s it made up over a third of all export revenues,[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-RWT_export_evolution-251) but since then its price has steadily dropped relative to other commodities,[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-290) and wool is no longer profitable for many farmers.[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-291) In contrast, [dairy farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_farming_in_New_Zealand "Dairy farming in New Zealand") increased, with the number of dairy cows doubling between 1990 and 2007,[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-292) to become New Zealand's largest export earner.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-293) In the year to June 2018, dairy products accounted for 17.7% ($14.1billion) of total exports,[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-GSTC_StatNZ-283) and the country's largest company, [Fonterra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fonterra "Fonterra"), controls almost one-third of the international dairy trade.[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-294) Other exports in 2017–18 were meat (8.8%), wood and wood products (6.2%), fruit (3.6%), machinery (2.2%) and wine (2.1%).[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-GSTC_StatNZ-283) [New Zealand's wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_wine "New Zealand wine") industry has followed a similar trend to dairy, the number of vineyards doubling over the same period,[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-295) overtaking wool exports for the first time in 2007.[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-296)[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-297)

### Infrastructure

In 2025, [renewable energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_New_Zealand "Renewable energy in New Zealand") reached a new record, generating 47.7% of [New Zealand's gross energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_New_Zealand "Energy in New Zealand") supply, up from 45.6% in 2024.[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-298) The majority of the [country's electricity supply](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_sector_in_New_Zealand "Electricity sector in New Zealand") is generated from [hydroelectric power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectric_power_in_New_Zealand "Hydroelectric power in New Zealand"), with major schemes on the [Waikato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waikato_River "Waikato River"), [Waitaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waitaki_River "Waitaki River") and [Clutha / Mata-Au](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clutha_River_/_Mata-Au "Clutha River / Mata-Au") rivers, as well as at [Manapouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manapouri_Power_Station "Manapouri Power Station"). [Geothermal power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_power_in_New_Zealand "Geothermal power in New Zealand") is also a significant generator of electricity, with several large stations located across the [Taupō Volcanic Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taup%C5%8D_Volcanic_Zone "Taupō Volcanic Zone") in the North Island. The four main companies in the generation and retail market are [Contact Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contact_Energy "Contact Energy"), [Genesis Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesis_Energy_Limited "Genesis Energy Limited"), [Mercury Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercury_Energy "Mercury Energy") and [Meridian Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian_Energy "Meridian Energy"). State-owned [Transpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transpower_New_Zealand "Transpower New Zealand") operates the high-voltage transmission grids in the North and South Islands, as well as the [Inter-Island HVDC link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HVDC_Inter-Island "HVDC Inter-Island") connecting the two together.[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Energy2015-299)

The provision of [water supply and sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_New_Zealand "Water supply and sanitation in New Zealand") is generally of good quality. Regional authorities provide water abstraction, treatment and distribution infrastructure to most developed areas.[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-300)[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-301)

[![A mid-size jet airliner in flight. The plane livery is all-black and features a New Zealand silver fern mark.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Air_New_Zealand%2C_Boeing_787-9_ZK-NZE_%27All_Blacks%27_NRT_%2827091961041%29.jpg/250px-Air_New_Zealand%2C_Boeing_787-9_ZK-NZE_%27All_Blacks%27_NRT_%2827091961041%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Air_New_Zealand,_Boeing_787-9_ZK-NZE_'All_Blacks'_NRT_(27091961041).jpg) A [Boeing 787-9 Dreamliner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_787_Dreamliner "Boeing 787 Dreamliner") of [Air New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_New_Zealand "Air New Zealand"), the flag carrier of New Zealand

[New Zealand's transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_New_Zealand "Transport in New Zealand") network comprises 94,000 kilometres (58,410mi) of roads, including 199 kilometres (124mi) of motorways,[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-302) and 4,128 kilometres (2,565mi) of railway lines.[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-CIA-102) Most major cities and towns are linked by bus services, although the private car is the predominant mode of transport.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-303) The [country's railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_New_Zealand "Rail transport in New Zealand") were privatised in 1993 but were re-nationalised by the government in stages between 2004 and 2008. The state-owned enterprise [KiwiRail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KiwiRail "KiwiRail") now operates the railways, with the exception of commuter services in Auckland and Wellington, which are operated by [Auckland One Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auckland_One_Rail "Auckland One Rail") and [Transdev Wellington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transdev_Wellington "Transdev Wellington") respectively.[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-304) Railways run the length of the country, although most lines now carry freight rather than passengers.[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-305) The road and rail networks in the two main islands are linked by [roll-on/roll-off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roll-on/roll-off "Roll-on/roll-off") ferries between Wellington and [Picton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picton,_New_Zealand "Picton, New Zealand"), operated by [Interislander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interislander "Interislander") (part of KiwiRail) and [Bluebridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/StraitNZ "StraitNZ"). Most international visitors arrive via air.[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-306) New Zealand has [four international airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_New_Zealand "List of airports in New Zealand"): [Auckland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auckland_Airport "Auckland Airport"), [Christchurch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christchurch_Airport "Christchurch Airport"), [Queenstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queenstown_Airport "Queenstown Airport") and [Wellington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellington_Airport "Wellington Airport"). Only Auckland and Christchurch have runways long enough for aircraft capable of long haul flights to Asia and the Americas.[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-307)

The [New Zealand Post Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Post_Office "New Zealand Post Office") had a monopoly over [telecommunications in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_New_Zealand "Telecommunications in New Zealand") until 1987 when [Telecom New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spark_New_Zealand "Spark New Zealand") was formed, initially as a state-owned enterprise and then privatised in 1990.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Wilson2010-308) [Chorus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chorus_Limited "Chorus Limited"), which was split from Telecom (now Spark) in 2011,[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-309) still owns the majority of the telecommunications infrastructure, but competition from other providers has increased.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Wilson2010-308) A large-scale rollout of gigabit-capable [fibre to the premises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fibre_to_the_premises "Fibre to the premises"), branded as [Ultra-Fast Broadband](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultra-Fast_Broadband "Ultra-Fast Broadband"), began in 2009 with a target of being available to 87% of the population by 2022.[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-310)As of 2017[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=New_Zealand&action=edit), the United Nations [International Telecommunication Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Telecommunication_Union "International Telecommunication Union") ranks New Zealand 13th in the development of information and communications infrastructure.[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-311)

### Science and technology

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Ernest_Rutherford_1932.jpg/250px-Ernest_Rutherford_1932.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ernest_Rutherford_1932.jpg) New Zealand physicist [Ernest Rutherford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_Rutherford "Ernest Rutherford") has been described as "the father of nuclear physics".

The accumulation of knowledge of agricultural practice and the effects of herbal remedies in the treatment of illness and disease by Māori _[tohunga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tohunga "Tohunga")_ as well as their precise observation of nature might be considered an indigenous contribution to science in New Zealand. [Cook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cook "James Cook")'s voyages in the 1700s and [Darwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Darwin "Charles Darwin")'s in 1835 had important scientific botanical and zoological objectives.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Science_New_Zealand-312) The establishment of universities in the 19th century fostered scientific discoveries by notable New Zealanders including [Ernest Rutherford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_Rutherford "Ernest Rutherford") for splitting the atom, [William Pickering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Pickering_(rocket_scientist) "Bill Pickering (rocket scientist)") for rocket science, [Maurice Wilkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurice_Wilkins "Maurice Wilkins") for helping discover DNA, [Beatrice Tinsley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatrice_Tinsley "Beatrice Tinsley") for galaxy formation, [Archibald McIndoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archibald_McIndoe "Archibald McIndoe") for plastic surgery, [Sydney Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Smith_(pathologist) "Sydney Smith (pathologist)") for forensics and [Alan MacDiarmid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_MacDiarmid "Alan MacDiarmid") for conducting polymers.[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-New_Zealand_Herald-313)

[Crown Research Institutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Research_Institute "Crown Research Institute") (CRIs) were formed in 1992 from existing government-owned research organisations. Their role is to research and develop new science, knowledge, products and services across the economic, environmental, social and cultural spectrum for the benefit of New Zealand.[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-314) The total gross expenditure on [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development "Research and development") (R&D) as a proportion of GDP rose to 1.37% in 2018, up from 1.23% in 2015. New Zealand ranks 21st in the OECD for its gross R&D spending as a percentage of GDP.[\[307\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-315) New Zealand was ranked 26th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index "Global Innovation Index") in 2025.[\[308\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-316)[\[309\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-317)

The [New Zealand Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Space_Agency "New Zealand Space Agency") was created by the government in 2016 for space policy, regulation and sector development. [Rocket Lab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_Lab "Rocket Lab") was the first commercial rocket launcher in the country.[\[310\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-318)

Most private and commercial research organisations in New Zealand focus on the agricultural and fisheries sectors. Examples include the [Cawthron Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cawthron_Institute "Cawthron Institute"), the [Livestock Improvement Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livestock_Improvement_Corporation "Livestock Improvement Corporation"), the [Fonterra Research and Development Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fonterra#Fonterra_Research_and_Development_Centre "Fonterra"), the [Bragato Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Winegrowers#Bragato_Research_Institute "New Zealand Winegrowers"), the [Kiwifruit Breeding Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zespri#Kiwifruit_Breeding_Centre "Zespri"), and [B+LNZ Genetics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beef_+_Lamb_New_Zealand#B+LNZ_Genetics "Beef + Lamb New Zealand").

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_New_Zealand "Demographics of New Zealand") and [List of cities in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_New_Zealand "List of cities in New Zealand")

[![Stationary population pyramid broken down into 21 age ranges.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/New_Zealandpop.svg/330px-New_Zealandpop.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:New_Zealandpop.svg) [Population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid "Population pyramid") (2017)

The [2023 New Zealand census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_New_Zealand_census "2023 New Zealand census") enumerated a resident population of 4,993,923, an increase of 6.3% over the [2018 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_New_Zealand_census "2018 New Zealand census") figure.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-Census2023PopCounts-5) As of September2026, the total population has risen to an estimated 5,357,280.[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-populationestimate-14) New Zealand's population increased at a rate of 1.9% per year in the seven years ended June 2020. In September 2020 [Statistics New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statistics_New_Zealand "Statistics New Zealand") reported that the population had climbed above 5 million people in September 2019, according to population estimates based on the 2018 census.[\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-population1-319)[\[n 9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-321)

New Zealand's population today is concentrated to the north of the country, with around 76.4% of the population living in the North Island and 23.6% in the South Island as of June 2025.[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-NZ_population_data_2018-322) During the 20th century, New Zealand's population [drifted north](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drift_to_the_north "Drift to the north"). In 1921, the country's [median centre of population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_of_population "Center of population") was in the Tasman Sea west of [Levin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levin,_New_Zealand "Levin, New Zealand") in [Manawatū-Whanganui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manawat%C5%AB-Whanganui "Manawatū-Whanganui"); by 2017, it had moved 280km (170mi) north to near [Kawhia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawhia_Harbour "Kawhia Harbour") in Waikato.[\[314\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-323)

New Zealand is a predominantly urban country, with 84.5% of the population living in [urban areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_areas_of_New_Zealand "Urban areas of New Zealand") and 51.4% in the seven cities with populations exceeding 100,000.[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-NZ_population_data_2018-322) [Auckland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auckland "Auckland"), with over 1.4million residents, is by far the largest city.[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-NZ_population_data_2018-322) New Zealand cities generally rank highly on international livability measures. For instance, in 2024, Auckland was ranked the world's fifth [most liveable city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Most_liveable_city "Most liveable city") and Wellington the fourteenth by the Mercer Quality of Living Survey.[\[315\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-324)

The [median age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Median_age "Median age") of the New Zealand population at the 2023 census was 38.1 years, up from 37.4 years in the 2018 census[\[316\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-325)[\[317\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-326) with life expectancy in 2022–2024 being 80.1 years for males and 83.5 years for females.[\[318\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-327) While New Zealand is experiencing [sub-replacement fertility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-replacement_fertility "Sub-replacement fertility"), with a total fertility rate of 1.6 in 2020, the fertility rate is above the OECD average.[\[319\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-328)[\[320\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-329) By 2050, the median age is projected to rise to 43 years and the percentage of people 60 years of age and older to rise from 18% to 29%.[\[321\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand#cite_note-UNtwspop-330)
